<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-33BA3A42-D90E-400D-A934-CADF6E6B81F9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$2$ packets of crisps and $2$ drinks cost $\pounds 1.50$. $2$ packets of crisps and $1$ drink costs $\pounds 1.10$. How much would $4$ packets of crisps and $3$ drinks cost? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of a packet of crisp $= a$

Let the price of a drink $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
2a + 2b + 2a + b  &= 1.50 + 1.10 \\\\
4a + 3b           &= \pounds 2.60
\end {aligned}
$

Cost of $4$ packets of crisps and $3$ drinks $= \pounds 2.60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the price of a packet of crisp $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the price of a drink $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
2a + 2b + 2a + b  &= 1.50 + 1.10 \\\\
4a + 3b           &= \pounds 2.60
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of $4$ packets of crisps and $3$ drinks $= \pounds 2.60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B40C9067-5309-4BDD-A6D3-04C7A850101A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

I think of a number, multiply it by $2$ and subtract $5$. The answer is $11$. What is my number? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 - 5    &= 11 \\\\
2a                &= 11 + 5 \\\\
2a                &= 16 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {16} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 8} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 8} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 - 5    &= 11 \\\\
2a                &= 11 + 5 \\\\
2a                &= 16 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {16} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 8} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 8} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0614640E-B41A-4826-860D-7235E6EC27FC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sophie should have divided a number by $4$, but instead she subtracted $4$. She got the answer $88$. What should her answer have been?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 4 &= 88 \\\\
a     &= 88 + 4\\\\
a     &= 92
\end{aligned}
$

Answer should have been:

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {a} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {92} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 23} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 23} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
&= 23
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$23$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a - 4 &= 88 \\\\
a     &= 88 + 4\\\\
a     &= 92
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Answer should have been:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {a} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {92} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 23} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 23} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
&= 23
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$23$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-83110D7D-4A15-444F-BC4E-DC7DC2E96129'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

When two numbers are added together, the result is $32$. When the smaller number is subtracted from the larger one, the result is $6$. 
Find the numbers. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the larger number $= a$

Let the smaller number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b &= 32 \\\\
a - b &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$

Adding the above two equations we get,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b + a - b &= 32 + 6 \\\\
2 a           &= 38 \\\\
a             &= \dfrac{38}{2} \\\\
a             &= \dfrac{2 \times 19}{2} \\\\
a             &= \dfrac{\cancel {2} \times 19} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a             &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Larger number}   &= 19 \\\\
\text {Smaller number}  &= 19 - 6 \\\\
                        &= 13
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the larger number $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the smaller number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + b &= 32 \\\\
a - b &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Adding the above two equations we get,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + b + a - b &= 32 + 6 \\\\
2 a           &= 38 \\\\
a             &= \dfrac{38}{2} \\\\
a             &= \dfrac{2 \times 19}{2} \\\\
a             &= \dfrac{\cancel {2} \times 19} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a             &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Larger number}   &= 19 \\\\
\text {Smaller number}  &= 19 - 6 \\\\
                        &= 13
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-682CC1A0-4A88-4DD0-BD42-0767DA7FEE5D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Eggs are sold in boxes of $12$, costing $\pounds 1.68$. I wish to make $15$ cakes for a local fete and the recipe requires that 
I use $5$ eggs for each cake. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many eggs will I use altogether if I make $15$ cakes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text {Cake}        &= 5 \ \text {Eggs} \\\\
1 \times 15             &= 5 \times 15 \\\\
15 \ \text {Cakes}      &= 75 \ \text {Eggs}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text {Cake}        &= 5 \ \text {Eggs} \\\\
1 \times 15             &= 5 \times 15 \\\\
15 \ \text {Cakes}      &= 75 \ \text {Eggs}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$75$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many boxes must I buy to ensure I have enough eggs to make 
the $15$ cakes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text {Eggs}       &= 1 \ \text {Box} \\\\
1 \ \text {Egg}         &= \dfrac {1} {12} \ \text {Box} \\\\
1 \times 75             &= \dfrac {1} {12} \times 75 \\\\
75                      &= \dfrac {75} {12} \\\\
75                      &= 6 \ \text {remainder} \ 3 \\\\
75 \ \text {Eggs}       &\approx 7 \ \text {Boxes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text {Eggs}       &= 1 \ \text {Box} \\\\
1 \ \text {Egg}         &= \dfrac {1} {12} \ \text {Box} \\\\
1 \times 75             &= \dfrac {1} {12} \times 75 \\\\
75                      &= \dfrac {75} {12} \\\\
75                      &= 6 \ \text {remainder} \ 3 \\\\
75 \ \text {Eggs}       &\approx 7 \ \text {Boxes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much will I pay for the eggs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost}      &= 7 \times \pounds 1.68 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 11.76
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 11.76$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost}      &= 7 \times \pounds 1.68 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 11.76
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 11.76$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C5BEA94E-1A7E-437A-B7E1-A81049B5F92C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Verity thinks of a number. She multiplies it by 7 then adds 8. If the resulting number did she think of in the first place? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 7 + 8  &= 71 \\\\
7a              &= 71 - 8 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{63}{7} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{7 \times 9}{7} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{\cancel 7 \times 9}{\cancel 7} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 7 + 8  &= 71 \\\\
7a              &= 71 - 8 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{63}{7} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{7 \times 9}{7} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{\cancel 7 \times 9}{\cancel 7} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-714A3922-27FE-4977-8C4F-8B64886AF8C2'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

**A triangle has area** *$24cm^2$* . It's base is $6\,cm$.What is its height?   

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the height of the triangle $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Area}                        &= 24 \ \text {cm}^2 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times 6 \times h    &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {6h} {2}                     &= 24 \\\\
6h                                  &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
h                                   &= \dfrac {24 \times 2} {6} \\\\
h                                   &= \dfrac {4 \times 6 \times 2} {6} \\\\
h                                   &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {6} \times 2} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
h                                   &= 4 \times 2 \\\\
h                                   &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$

Height of the triangle $= 8 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the height of the triangle $= h$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Area}                        &= 24 \ \text {cm}^2 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times 6 \times h    &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {6h} {2}                     &= 24 \\\\
6h                                  &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
h                                   &= \dfrac {24 \times 2} {6} \\\\
h                                   &= \dfrac {4 \times 6 \times 2} {6} \\\\
h                                   &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {6} \times 2} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
h                                   &= 4 \times 2 \\\\
h                                   &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Height of the triangle $= 8 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7D2466F0-AE57-429A-BA31-12DECBC77D48'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Write in the missing number to make this correct:

$0.627 = 0.6 + 0.02 + \ldots$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
0.627       &= 0.60 + 0.02 + a \\\\
0.627       &= 0.62 + a \\\\
a           &= 0.627 - 0.620 \\\\
a           &= 0.007
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 0.007$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.007$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$

$

0.627       &= 0.62 + a \\\\
a           &= 0.627 - 0.620 \\\\
a           &= 0.007
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The missing number $= 0.007$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DFD8D8D5-83F1-4AA0-B9E2-0ED508142867'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Halid makes a sequence of $5$ numbers. The first number is $2$. The last number is $18$. 
His rule is to add the same amount each time.

Write in the missing numbers:

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Halid adds the same amount each time. The difference between the consecutive terms of the sequence is always same.

Let take the amount $= a$. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+a)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+a)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+a)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+a)}} \\
2,  &&    \ldots ,  &&  \ldots ,  &&   \ldots ,  &&   18
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
2 + 4a      &= 18 \\\\
4a          &= 18 - 2 \\\\
4a          &= 16 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a           &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The amount Halid adds $= 4$

Hence the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+4)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+4)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+4)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
{(+4)}} \\
2,  &&    \underline {6} ,  &&  \underline {10} ,  &&  \underline {14} ,  &&   18
\end{matrix}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6, 10, 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
Halid adds the same amount each time. The difference between the consecutive terms of the sequence is always same.
<emptyline>
Let take the amount $= a$. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+a)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+a)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+a)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+a)}} \\
2,  &&    \ldots ,  &&  \ldots ,  &&   \ldots ,  &&   18
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 + 4a      &= 18 \\\\
4a          &= 18 - 2 \\\\
4a          &= 16 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a           &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The amount Halid adds $= 4$
<emptyline>
Hence the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+4)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+4)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+4)}}
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info{(+4)}} \\
2,  &&    \underline {6} ,  &&  \underline {10} ,  &&  \underline {14} ,  &&   18
\end{matrix}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6, 10, 14$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B51C61A9-BBFD-4FCA-9F5F-BB543CC361E8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Alysha and Julia have some biscuits. Altogether they have $14$ biscuits.  Alysha has $2$ more biscuits than Julia.  

How many biscuits do Alysha and Julia each have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of biscuits Julia has $= a$

Number of biscuits Alysha has $= a + 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 2   &= 14 \\\\
2a          &= 14 - 2 \\\\
2a          &= 12 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of biscuits Julia has}      &= 6 \\\\
\text {Number of biscuits Alysha has}     &= 6 + 2 \\\\
                                          &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of biscuits Julia has $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of biscuits Alysha has $= a + 2$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 2   &= 14 \\\\
2a          &= 14 - 2 \\\\
2a          &= 12 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of biscuits Julia has}      &= 6 \\\\
\text {Number of biscuits Alysha has}     &= 6 + 2 \\\\
                                          &= 8
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7B74D5EE-ABDD-4862-9E72-635714D53045'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Jemma thinks of a number. She says, 

"Add $3$ to my number and then multiply the result by $5$. The answer is $35$." 

What is Jemma's number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 3) \times 5  &= 35 \\\\
5a + 3 \times 5   &= 35 \\\\
5a + 15           &= 35 \\\\
5a                &= 35 - 15 \\\\
5a                &= 20 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {20} {5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Jemma's number $= 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 3) \times 5  &= 35 \\\\
5a + 3 \times 5   &= 35 \\\\
5a + 15           &= 35 \\\\
5a                &= 35 - 15 \\\\
5a                &= 20 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {20} {5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Jemma's number $= 4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-786ECD30-5585-4FE5-91C7-28D8FFC8B3AE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sapna makes a fruit salad using bananas, oranges and apples.

For every one banana, she uses $2$ oranges and $3$ apples. 

Sapna uses $24$ fruits.

How many oranges does she use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of bananas used $= a$

Number of oranges used $= 2a$

Number of apples used $= 3a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + 3a &= 24 \\\\
6a          &= 24 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times 6} {6} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a           &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of bananas used}      &= 4 \\\\
\text {Number of oranges used}      &= 2a \\\\
                                    &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
                                    &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of bananas used $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of oranges used $= 2a$
<emptyline>
Number of apples used $= 3a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + 3a &= 24 \\\\
6a          &= 24 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times 6} {6} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a           &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of bananas used}      &= 4 \\\\
\text {Number of oranges used}      &= 2a \\\\
                                    &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
                                    &= 8
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9170FD6C-D8D5-431A-80F7-F4C30ABB5FEF'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

I am thinking of a number that when I multiply it by two and divide the result by four, then multiply the result of that by itself, gives me a number that when I subtract four and divide by ten, gives me six. What is the number that I originally thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {\dfrac {a \times 2} {4} \times \dfrac {a \times 2} {4} - 4 } {10}                                           &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {2 \times a} {2 \times 2} \times \dfrac {2 \times a} {2 \times 2} - 4                                        &= 6 \times 10 \\\\
\dfrac {\cancel{2} \times a} {\cancel{2} \times 2} \times \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times a} {\cancel{2} \times 2} - 4    &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times a} {2 \times 2}                                                                                    &= 60 + 4 \\\\
\dfrac {a^2} {4}                                                                                                    &= 64 \\\\
a^2                                                                                                                 &= 64 \times 4 \\\\
a^2                                                                                                                 &= 256 \\\\
a                                                                                                                   &= \sqrt {256} \\\\
a                                                                                                                   &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {\dfrac {a \times 2} {4} \times \dfrac {a \times 2} {4} - 4 } {10}                                           &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {2 \times a} {2 \times 2} \times \dfrac {2 \times a} {2 \times 2} - 4                                        &= 6 \times 10 \\\\
\dfrac {\cancel{2} \times a} {\cancel{2} \times 2} \times \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times a} {\cancel{2} \times 2} - 4    &= 60 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times a} {2 \times 2}                                                                                    &= 60 + 4 \\\\
\dfrac {a^2} {4}                                                                                                    &= 64 \\\\
a^2                                                                                                                 &= 64 \times 4 \\\\
a^2                                                                                                                 &= 256 \\\\
a                                                                                                                   &= \sqrt {256} \\\\
a                                                                                                                   &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 16$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$16$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-59DED6AF-072C-4471-8B10-931ECD8F050E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Three apples and a banana cost $32 \text{p}$. Six apples and a banana cost $53 \text{p}$. How much does one banana cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of an apple $= a$

Cost of a banana $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
3a + b  &= 32 \text {p} \\\\
b       &= 32 - 3a
\end {aligned}
$

Using the above calcuation we get,

$
\begin{aligned}
6a + b  &= 53 \text {p} \\\\
6a + 32 - 3a    &= 53 \\\\
3a              &= 53 - 32 \\\\
3a              &= 21 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {21} {3} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 7} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a               &= 7 \text {p}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cost of an apple}    &= 7 \text {p} \\\\
\text {Cost of one banana}  &= 32 - 3a \\\\
                            &= 32 - 3 \times 7 \\\\
                            &= 32 - 21 \\\\
                            &= 11 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of an apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of a banana $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
3a + b  &= 32 \text {p} \\\\
b       &= 32 - 3a
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using the above calcuation we get,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
6a + b  &= 53 \text {p} \\\\
6a + 32 - 3a    &= 53 \\\\
3a              &= 53 - 32 \\\\
3a              &= 21 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {21} {3} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 7} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a               &= 7 \text {p}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Cost of an apple}    &= 7 \text {p} \\\\
\text {Cost of one banana}  &= 32 - 3a \\\\
                            &= 32 - 3 \times 7 \\\\
                            &= 32 - 21 \\\\
                            &= 11 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11 \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-59782F67-CD3A-4562-8A32-D1EC35290808'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

After a card game, four gamblers had $\pounds 234$ between them. Harry had $\pounds 20$ more than Dana, 
Charlotte had $\pounds 53$ more than Dana and Sid had $\pounds 71$ more than Dana.

How much did each of them have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the money Dana had (in $\pounds$) $= a$

Money Harry had $= a + 20$

Money Charlotte had $= a + 53$

Money Sid had $= a + 71$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 20 + a + 53 + a + 71  &= \pounds 234 \\\\
4a + 144                      &= 234 \\\\
4a                            &= 234 - 144 \\\\
4a                            &= 90 \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {90} {4} \\\\
a                             &= \pounds 22.50
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Money Dana had}        &= \pounds 22.50 \\\\
\text {Money Harry had}       &= 22.50 + 20 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 42.50 \\\\
\text {Money Charlotte had}   &= 22.50 + 53 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 75.50 \\\\
\text {Money Sid had}         &= 22.50 + 71 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 93.50
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Dana $=$ $\pounds 22.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Harry $=$ $\pounds 42.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Charlotte $=$ $\pounds 75.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sid $=$ $\pounds 93.50$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}
$

$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The letters on the $7$ cards below have a number $1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6$ or $7$ on the back, but not in that order.

$\boxed{S} \ \boxed{t} \ \boxed{P} \ \boxed{a} \ \boxed{u} \ \boxed{l} \ \boxed{s}$

If $\boxed{s}$  has the number $1$ one the back and the cards

$\boxed{s} \ \boxed{a} \ \boxed{t}$ add up to $8$,

$\boxed{s} \ \boxed{a} \ \boxed{P}$ add up to $9$,

$\boxed{l} \ \boxed{a} \ \boxed{P}$ add up to $11$.

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
6 \times b  &= 96 \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {96} {6} \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {6 \times 16} {6} \\\\
b           &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 16} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
b           &= 16
\end {aligned}
$

The Missing number $= 16$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B7931AAD-CC40-434A-B67C-9F5D390398BC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The sum of two numbers is $250$. 

Write in the missing digits in the boxes:

$\boxed{7} \boxed{\phantom{1}} +  \boxed{\phantom{1}}\boxed{\phantom{1}}\boxed{6} = \boxed{2} \boxed{5} \boxed{0}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccc}
    &       &   7   &   4 \\
{}+ &   1   &   8   &   6 \\
\hline
    &   2   &   5   &   0
\end{array}
$

$\boxed{7} \boxed{4} +  \boxed{1}\boxed{8}\boxed{6} = \boxed{2} \boxed{5} \boxed{0}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\boxed{7} \boxed{4} +  \boxed{1}\boxed{8}\boxed{6} = \boxed{2} \boxed{5} \boxed{0}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{array}{cccccc}
    &       &   7   &   4 \\
{}+ &   1   &   8   &   6 \\
\hline
    &   2   &   5   &   0
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
$\boxed{7} \boxed{4} +  \boxed{1}\boxed{8}\boxed{6} = \boxed{2} \boxed{5} \boxed{0}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\boxed{7} \boxed{4} +  \boxed{1}\boxed{8}\boxed{6} = \boxed{2} \boxed{5} \boxed{0}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E095A1B1-A5D7-4A9A-9C71-AB7E49731549'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mary, Jess and Jo run a race. 

Jess's time is $18 \ \text{seconds}$. 

Jo finishes $6 \ \text{seconds}$ before Jess. 

Mary finishes $4 \ \text{seconds}$ after Jo. 

What is **Mary's time** in seconds?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time taken by Jess}  &= 18 \ \text {seconds} \\\\
\text {Time taken by Jo}    &= 18 - 6 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text {seconds} \\\\
\text {Time taken by Mary}  &= 12 + 4 \\\\
                            &= 16 \ \text {seconds}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16 \ \text {seconds}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time taken by Jess}  &= 18 \ \text {seconds} \\\\
\text {Time taken by Jo}    &= 18 - 6 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text {seconds} \\\\
\text {Time taken by Mary}  &= 12 + 4 \\\\
                            &= 16 \ \text {seconds}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$16 \ \text {seconds}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-ED0E72A9-DD21-4A7F-9701-21CDEA62D691'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A shop sells apples and oranges.

Kate buys  $1$ apple and $2$ oranges. She paid $48 \text{p}$.

Jade buys  $1$ apple and $1$ orange. She paid $31 \text{p}$.

How much does $1$ orange cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of an apple $= a$

Let the cost of an orange $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2b  &= 48 \text {p} \\\\
a + b   &= 31 \text {p}
\end{aligned}
$

Subtracting the above two equations we get,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + 2b - a - b  &= 48 - 31 \\\\
b               &= 17 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ orange $= 17 \text {p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17 \text {p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of an apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of an orange $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2b  &= 48 \text {p} \\\\
a + b   &= 31 \text {p}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Subtracting the above two equations we get,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + 2b - a - b  &= 48 - 31 \\\\
b               &= 17 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of $1$ orange $= 17 \text {p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17 \text {p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4D8E25F8-9D3B-41E4-A587-01F4188FD3F2'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Alysha and Julia each have some money. 

Altogether they have $\pounds 3.20$. 

Alysha gives Julia $20 \text{p}$ so that they both have the same amount. 

How much money did each have at the start?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Let the money Alysha had (in $\pounds$) $= a$

Let the money Julia had (in $\pounds$) $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a   &= b + 20 \text {p} \\\\
a   &= b + \pounds 0.20
\end {aligned}
$

Using this we get,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b           &= \pounds 3.20 \\\\
b + 0.20 + b    &= 3.20 \\\\
2b + 0.20       &= 3.20 \\\\
2b              &= 3.20 - 0.20 \\\\
2b              &= 3 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
b               &= \pounds 1.50
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Julia had}   &= \pounds 1.50 \\\\
\text {Alysha had}  &= 1.50 + 0.20 \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1.70
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
Let the money Alysha had (in $\pounds$) $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the money Julia had (in $\pounds$) $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a   &= b + 20 \text {p} \\\\
a   &= b + \pounds 0.20
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using this we get,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + b           &= \pounds 3.20 \\\\
b + 0.20 + b    &= 3.20 \\\\
2b + 0.20       &= 3.20 \\\\
2b              &= 3.20 - 0.20 \\\\
2b              &= 3 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
b               &= \pounds 1.50
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Julia had}   &= \pounds 1.50 \\\\
\text {Alysha had}  &= 1.50 + 0.20 \\\\
                    &= \pounds 1.70
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.50$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B09AF32E-2996-4090-B6BE-24F80181CD57'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If I treble a number and add $6$, I get the same answer as when I add $16$ to that number. What is my original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 3 + 6      &= a + 16 \\\\
3a - a              &= 16 - 6 \\\\
2a                  &= 10 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 5} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 5} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                   &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 3 + 6      &= a + 16 \\\\
3a - a              &= 16 - 6 \\\\
2a                  &= 10 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 5} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 5} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                   &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E04A5D82-8409-45E4-B659-1A199BE3C232'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

My money box contains an equal number of $5 \text{p}, 10 \text{p}$ and $20 \text{p}$ coins and no other coins. 
How many coins are there altogether if there is $\pounds 4.90$ in the money box?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Let the total number of coins $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {3} \times 5 \text {p} + \dfrac {c} {3} \times 10 \text {p} + \dfrac {c} {3} \times 20 \text {p}     &= \pounds 4.90 \\\\
\dfrac {5c} {3} + \dfrac {10c} {3} + \dfrac {20c} {3}                                                           &= 490 \text {p} \\\\
\dfrac {5c + 10c + 20c} {3}                                                                                     &= 490 \\\\
35c                                                                                                             &= 490 \times 3 \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= \dfrac {490 \times 3} {35} \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= \dfrac {14 \times 35 \times 3} {35} \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= \dfrac {14 \times \cancel {35} \times 3} {\cancel {35}} \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= 14 \times 3 \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= 42
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of coins $= 42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
Let the total number of coins $= c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {3} \times 5 \text {p} + \dfrac {c} {3} \times 10 \text {p} + \dfrac {c} {3} \times 20 \text {p}     &= \pounds 4.90 \\\\
\dfrac {5c} {3} + \dfrac {10c} {3} + \dfrac {20c} {3}                                                           &= 490 \text {p} \\\\
\dfrac {5c + 10c + 20c} {3}                                                                                     &= 490 \\\\
35c                                                                                                             &= 490 \times 3 \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= \dfrac {490 \times 3} {35} \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= \dfrac {14 \times 35 \times 3} {35} \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= \dfrac {14 \times \cancel {35} \times 3} {\cancel {35}} \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= 14 \times 3 \\\\
c                                                                                                               &= 42
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of coins $= 42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$42$
</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Winston thinks of two different whole numbers that add together to make $20$ and multiply to give $84$. What are his two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$a \times b = 84$

$a + b = 20$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- Both the numbers should be smaller than $20$
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $84$ 
- The sum of the $2$ numbers is $20$.

Factors of $84 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 12, 14, 21, 28, 42, 84$

Factor pairs of $84$ that are smaller than $20 = 6, 14 \ \text {and} \ 7, 12$

| Factors   |  Sum  | Numbers $< 20$    |
|:-------:  |:----: |:---------------:  |
| $6, 14$   | $20$  |       Yes         |
| $7, 12$   | $19$  |       Yes         |

A quick check shows that $6$ and $14$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers which Winston thinks $= 6,14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the second number $= b$
<emptyline>

|:-------:  |:----: |:---------------:  |

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

He then thinks of a new number, multiplies it by $9$ and subtracts $15$. The result is $66$. What was his original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the new number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 9 - 15     &= 66 \\\\
a \times 9          &= 66 + 15 \\\\
a \times 9          &= 81 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {81} {9} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 9} {9} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{9} \times 9} {\cancel{9}} \\\\
a                   &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The new number $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the new number $= a$

a                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 9} {9} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{9} \times 9} {\cancel{9}} \\\\
a                   &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The new number $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

There are $3$ moos in a quack and $13$ quacks in an oink.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many moos are there in $3$ quacks and $1$ oink?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \ \text {quacks} + 1 \ \text {oink} \\\\
&= 3 \ \text {quacks} + 13 \ \text {quacks} \\\\
&= 3 \times 3 \ \text {moos} + 3 \times 13 \ \text {moos} \\\\
&= 9 + 39 \\\\
&= 48 \ \text {moos}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \ \text{moos}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \ \text {quacks} + 1 \ \text {oink} \\\\
&= 3 \ \text {quacks} + 13 \ \text {quacks} \\\\
&= 3 \times 3 \ \text {moos} + 3 \times 13 \ \text {moos} \\\\
&= 9 + 39 \\\\
&= 48 \ \text {moos}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \ \text{moos}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of an oink does $9$ moos represent?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text {quack}           &= \dfrac {1} {13} \ \text {oink} \\\\
3 \ \text {moos}            &= \dfrac {1} {13} \ \text {oink} \\\\
3 \times 3 \ \text {moos}   &= 3 \times \dfrac {1} {13} \ \text {oink} \\\\
9 \ \text {moos}            &= \dfrac {3} {13} \ \text {oink}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {3} {13}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text {quack}           &= \dfrac {1} {13} \ \text {oink} \\\\
3 \ \text {moos}            &= \dfrac {1} {13} \ \text {oink} \\\\
3 \times 3 \ \text {moos}   &= 3 \times \dfrac {1} {13} \ \text {oink} \\\\
9 \ \text {moos}            &= \dfrac {3} {13} \ \text {oink}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {3} {13}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$800$ moos are sorted into piles $2$ oinks high. How many complete piles will there be and how many moos and quacks will be left over?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of piles}    &= \dfrac{\text{Total number of Moos}}{\text{Number of moos per pile}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{800}{2 \times 13 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{2 \times 400}{2 \times 13 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{\cancel{2} \times 400}{\cancel{2} \times 13 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{400}{39} \\\\
                                &= 10 \dfrac{10}{39} \\\\
                                &= 10 \ \text{piles} + 10 \ \text{Moos} \\\\
\text{Total number of piles}    &= 10 \ \text{piles} + 3 \ \text{Quacks} + 1 \ \text{Moos}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text{piles} + 3 \ \text{Quacks} + 1 \ \text{Moos}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of piles}    &= \dfrac{\text{Total number of Moos}}{\text{Number of moos per pile}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{800}{2 \times 13 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{2 \times 400}{2 \times 13 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{\cancel{2} \times 400}{\cancel{2} \times 13 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{400}{39} \\\\
                                &= 10 \dfrac{10}{39} \\\\
                                &= 10 \ \text{piles} + 10 \ \text{Moos} \\\\
\text{Total number of piles}    &= 10 \ \text{piles} + 3 \ \text{Quacks} + 1 \ \text{Moos}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10 \ \text{piles} + 3 \ \text{Quacks} + 1 \ \text{Moos}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

The instruction `$a  b$' means multiply $a$ by $b$, then add on $a$. For example: 

$5  2 = 5 \times 2 + 5 = 10 + 5 = 15$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of 

$4  3 =$

$4  0 =$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 4 \ 3 \\\\
&= 4 \times 3 + 4 \\\\
&= 12 + 4 \\\\
&= 16
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 4 \ 0 \\\\
&= 4 \times 0 + 4 \\\\
&= 0 + 4 \\\\
&= 4
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>

$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}

&= 4 \times 0 + 4 \\\\

\end {aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the value of $b$ if $3  b = 15$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
3 \ b           &= 15 \\\\
3 \times b + 3  &= 15 \\\\
3b              &= 15 - 3 \\\\
3b              &= 12 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
b               &= 4
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
3 \ b           &= 15 \\\\
3 \times b + 3  &= 15 \\\\
3b              &= 15 - 3 \\\\
3b              &= 12 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
b               &= 4
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

TODOSUBQUESTION
What is the value of $a$ if $a  a = 20$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a \ a                                   &= 20 \\\\
a \times a + a                          &= 20 \\\\
a ^ 2 + a - 20                          &= 0 \\\\
a ^ 2 + 5a - 4a - 20                    &= 0 \\\\
a \times (a + 5) - 4 \times (a + 5)     &= 0 \\\\
(a - 4) \times (a + 5)                  &= 0
\end {aligned}
$

As per the above calcuations there are two possible values of a,

$
\begin {aligned}
a - 4   &= 0 \\\\
a       &= 4
\end {aligned}
$

Or 

$
\begin {aligned}
a + 5   &= 0 \\\\
a       &= -5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a \ a                                   &= 20 \\\\
a \times a + a                          &= 20 \\\\
a ^ 2 + a - 20                          &= 0 \\\\
a ^ 2 + 5a - 4a - 20                    &= 0 \\\\
a \times (a + 5) - 4 \times (a + 5)     &= 0 \\\\
(a - 4) \times (a + 5)                  &= 0
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the above calcuations there are two possible values of a,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a - 4   &= 0 \\\\
a       &= 4
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Or 
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + 5   &= 0 \\\\
a       &= -5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$(a  b)  c$ means work out $(a  b)$ first and then work out "your answer" $c$.

For example: 

$(5  2)  3$ means do $5  2 = 5 \times 2 + 5 = 15$ first, then work out
$15  3 = 15 \times 3 + 15 = 45 + 15 = 60 $

Work out $(2  3)  4 =$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= ((2)  (3)) 4 \\\\
&= (2 \times 3 + 2) 4 \\\\
&= (6 + 2) 4 \\\\
&= 8 \ 4 \\\\
&= 8 \times 4 + 4 \\\\
&= 32 + 4 \\\\
&= 36
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
&= ((2)  (3)) 4 \\\\
&= (2 \times 3 + 2) 4 \\\\
&= (6 + 2) 4 \\\\
&= 8 \ 4 \\\\
&= 8 \times 4 + 4 \\\\
&= 32 + 4 \\\\
&= 36
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-295FC725-C559-4A9A-B53F-530F83AAA2FC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

I think of a number $n$. 

$5n$ is more than $60$, but $n + 5$ is less than $20$. What are the possible values of $n$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5n                                              &> 60 \\\\
\dfrac  {5n} {5}                                &> \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
\dfrac {\cancel {5} \times n} {\cancel {5}}     &> \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
n                                               &> \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 12} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
n                                               &> 12
\end{aligned}
$

Also,

$
\begin{aligned}
n + 5       &< 20 \\\\
n + 5 - 5   &< 20 - 5 \\\\
n           &< 15
\end{aligned}
$

As per the above calcuations,

$12 < n < 15$

Hence the possible values of $n$ $= 13, 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5n                                              &> 60 \\\\
\dfrac  {5n} {5}                                &> \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
\dfrac {\cancel {5} \times n} {\cancel {5}}     &> \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
n                                               &> \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 12} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
n                                               &> 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Also,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
n + 5       &< 20 \\\\
n + 5 - 5   &< 20 - 5 \\\\
n           &< 15
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the above calcuations,
<emptyline>
$12 < n < 15$
<emptyline>
Hence the possible values of $n$ $= 13, 14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13$
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-6F7C552E-E005-44B5-8C86-47CAB7CF711F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A shop sells bananas and pears. 

Max buys $1$ banana and $2$ pears. He paid $94 \text{p}$. 

Emily buys $1$ banana and $1$ pear. She paid $62 \text{p}$. 

How much does $1$ banana cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of a banana (in $\text {p}$) $= a$

Let the cost of a pear (in $\text {p}$) $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2b  &= 94 \text {p} \\\\
a + b   &= 62 \text {p} \\\\
b       &= 62 - a
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + 2b                  &= 94 \\\\
a + 2 \times (62 - a)   &= 94 \\\\
a + 124 - 2a            &= 94 \\\\
{}-a                    &= 94 - 124 \\\\
{}-a                    &= -30 \\\\
\cancel {-} a           &= \cancel {-} 30 \\\\
a                       &= 30 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$

A banana cost $= 30 \text {p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of a banana (in $\text {p}$) $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of a pear (in $\text {p}$) $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2b  &= 94 \text {p} \\\\
a + b   &= 62 \text {p} \\\\
b       &= 62 - a
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + 2b                  &= 94 \\\\
a + 2 \times (62 - a)   &= 94 \\\\
a + 124 - 2a            &= 94 \\\\
{}-a                    &= 94 - 124 \\\\
{}-a                    &= -30 \\\\
\cancel {-} a           &= \cancel {-} 30 \\\\
a                       &= 30 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
A banana cost $= 30 \text {p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30 \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-BB2EEC2A-FFEA-4BE1-89E7-4509E48E88A8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Benjamin walks dogs to earn some money. The formula below can be used to work out his pay.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Benjamin worked all day on Monday. He walked $13$ dogs before lunch and $15$ dogs afterwards.

Work out Benjamin's pay on Monday.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Benjamin pay on monday}       &= \pounds 12 + \pounds 1.5 \times (13+12) \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 12 + \pounds 1.5 \times 25 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 12 + \pounds 37.5 \\\\
\text{Benjamin pay on monday}       &= \pounds 49.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 49.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Benjamin pay on monday}       &= \pounds 12 + \pounds 1.5 \times (13+12) \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 12 + \pounds 1.5 \times 25 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 12 + \pounds 37.5 \\\\
\text{Benjamin pay on monday}       &= \pounds 49.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 49.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DC7231D7-DE01-44F0-83B3-D79030A58E4C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

There are a number of coins on a table. One quarter of the coins show heads.

If I turn over two coins, then one third show heads. How many coins are there altogether?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of coins $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {3}                                                          &= \dfrac {c} {4} + 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c} {3} - \dfrac {c} {4}                                         &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {c \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c} {12} - \dfrac {3c} {12}                                     &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c - 3c} {12}                                                   &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c} {12}                                                         &= 2 \\\\
c                                                                       &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
c                                                                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

Number of coins on the table $= 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of coins $= c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {3}                                                          &= \dfrac {c} {4} + 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c} {3} - \dfrac {c} {4}                                         &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {c \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c} {12} - \dfrac {3c} {12}                                     &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c - 3c} {12}                                                   &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c} {12}                                                         &= 2 \\\\
c                                                                       &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
c                                                                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of coins on the table $= 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A802EBFF-9616-4373-A0C4-AC740A707CD7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

There are twenty gifts stacked up into four piles. 

The first pile has $3$ less than the second pile. 

The second pile has two more than the third pile. 

The fourth pile has twice as many as the second pile. 

How many gifts are in each pile?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the gifts in second pile $= a$

Gifts in first pile $= a - 3$

Gifts in third pile $= a - 2$

Gifts in fourth pile $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 3 + a + a - 2 + 2a  &= 20 \\\\
5a - 5                  &= 20 \\\\
5a                      &= 20 + 5 \\\\
5a                      &= 25 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {25} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 5} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Gifts in second pile}    &= 5 \\\\
\text {Gifts in first pile}     &= a - 3 \\\\
                                &= 5 - 3 \\\\
                                &= 2 \\\\
\text {Gifts in third pile}     &= a - 2 \\\\
                                &= 5 - 2 \\\\
                                &= 5 - 2 \\\\
                                &= 3 \\\\
\text {Gifts in fourth pile}    &= 2a \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 5 \\\\
                                &= 10
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>

$
<emptyline>

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

c. Using your answers from parts a. and b. find three numbers $A, B$ and $C$ such that

$
\begin{aligned}
A - B &= C\\ \text{and}\\
A^2 - B^2 &= C^3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$A - B = C$

$A^2 - B^2 = C^3$

$
\begin{aligned}
A^2 - B^2           &= C^3 \\\
(A + B)(A - B)      &= C^3 \\\\
(A + B) \times C    &= C^3 \\\\
(A + B)             &= \dfrac{C^3}{C} \\\\
A + B               &= C^2 \\\\
3 + 1               &= 2^2
\end{aligned}
$

A,B and C would be $3,1 \ \text{and} \ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3,1 \ \text{and} \ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$A - B = C$
<emptyline>
$A^2 - B^2 = C^3$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
A^2 - B^2           &= C^3 \\\
(A + B)(A - B)      &= C^3 \\\\
(A + B) \times C    &= C^3 \\\\
(A + B)             &= \dfrac{C^3}{C} \\\\
A + B               &= C^2 \\\\
3 + 1               &= 2^2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
A,B and C would be $3,1 \ \text{and} \ 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3,1 \ \text{and} \ 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-01AF2191-BF2A-4490-8FC3-7F0158A834D0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Tilly decides to count using a clock. 

She starts counting from $1$ in the normal way, so:

$1, 2, 3, 4 \ldots$

But when she gets to $12$, the count goes back to $1$, so she counts: $\ldots 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 1, 2, 3 \ldots$

So, for example, using this method of counting, $4 + 9 = 1$ and $10 + 5 = 3$. Similarly, $3 \times 5 = 3$ and $2 \times 13 = 2$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-813D165A-86E1-41F9-8602-4C825F1619A8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Julia thinks of a number. She multiplies it by $5$ and then subtracts $14$. The answer she gets is $46$. What number did she originally think of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 5 - 14     &= 46 \\\\
5a                  &= 46 + 14 \\\\
5a                  &= 60 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac{60}{5} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 12} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 5 - 14     &= 46 \\\\
5a                  &= 46 + 14 \\\\
5a                  &= 60 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac{60}{5} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 12} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
